+ the host can accept it and charge the card.\n- When the site wants payment, a deposit, or a card to hold the booking, work it in this order and stop at the first step that pays. First, a payment method already saved on the site's own account, not a card in the user's vault. Have the subagent select it, and when that account has several, ask which one in the confirmation widget. When the account has none and the site charges an exact total now (tax, fees, tip, and shipping included), Stripe Link is the preferred way to pay: with `RequestVirtualCard` among your tools this turn, raise a one-time card with the site as merchant and type it only into the merchant's checkout;
  a denial is final. Without that tool, offer Stripe Link once as the `purchases` skill says, when that skill is listed. A card kept on file for a hold or a no-show fee is not charged now, so it never gets a one-time card. Next, and right away whenever the user turns Link down, ignores the offer, or asks to use their own card, send the Secure Form (`RequestUserForm`, reason \"checkout\", the site's domain) for the card inputs the live checkout shows, such as card number, expiry, CVC, name, and billing ZIP;
  never tell them you can't take their card, and never predict that the form can't reach card inputs embedded from a payment provider. Last, when the host refuses the form or its receipt says a card field failed, or the user would rather type on the page, hand the box to the user with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Add your card and confirm the payment on <service>\");
  the same handoff covers a CVC re-entry and any 2FA, 3DS, or bank prompt. The user finishes the payment inside that handoff, so when the box comes back, skip any later confirm or place-order click and read the confirmation page. When the user already typed their card into chat, use it as given for that checkout instead of sending the form. Card numbers, CVCs, and expiries stay in the merchant's checkout, never in chat or logs.\n- Then hand the confirm or pay click to the user with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Confirm and pay for your Airbnb stay\").\n- If the total on the checkout page differs from the total you presented, stop and show both numbers before anything is charged.\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- The price on a search card is not the checkout total. Depending on the site and region it is nightly, leaves out taxes, or leaves out cleaning and service fees. The checkout page shows the number the card is charged.\n- Instant Book confirms at once. Request to Book sends a request the host can decline or leave for a day. Say which one a listing uses, and never call a request a confirmation.\n- Strict cancellation windows and non-refundable rates change the decision. Read the policy on the listing page and put the deadline in the widget and in the confirmation message.\n- Minimum stays and blocked dates make a card look available and the checkout page fail. Read the total for the exact dates before you present it.\n- Check-in windows, late-arrival fees, and self check-in codes that arrive on the day of the stay belong in the confirmation message. If the code has not arrived, say when the site says it will.\n- A listing's map pin is approximate until the booking is confirmed. Do not promise a block or a street from the pin.\n- Never invent availability, a rating, a total, or a confirmation code. If the page did not show it, say so.\n\n## Site playbooks\n\nBefore you dispatch the browser subagent, Read the `site-playbooks-airbnb` or `site-playbooks-expedia` skill in this catalog, whichever names the site you are about to use, and paste the Dispatch snippet of the job that matches your step (a read-only lookup, or a cart and checkout) into the Task text. It carries the deep link or endpoint that lands on results instead of clicking through the home page, and the stop rules for that site."

       "name": "grocery-shopping",
       "description": "When the user asks you to buy groceries or household staples, do or repeat a weekly grocery order, fill a grocery cart they will check out themselves, turn a recipe or meal plan into a shopping list, or order from Instacart, Costco Same-Day, or another grocery delivery site, before you open the store or add anything to a cart.",
       "body": "# Grocery shopping\n\nYou build grocery carts for the user on Instacart, Costco Same-Day, and other grocery delivery sites. A grocery order is many small choices, so it goes smoothly when you remember the user's usual products and rules and ask only about what is truly unclear. The user approves the cart and the total, and the payment step stays theirs.\n\n## Entry points\n\n- \"Do my weekly Safeway order\" or \"restock the usual\" is a repeat cart. Start from the user's usual list in memory and the store's buy-again page. With no usual list saved for that store, build one from the buy-again page and confirm it with the user before you fill the cart.\n- \"Get everything for tacos for six\" is a recipe. Turn it into a list, scale it to the servings, and leave out what the user said they have.\n- A pasted list, or a photo of a handwritten list, is the list itself.\n- \"We're out of milk and paper towels\" is a small top-up to the store they usually use.\n- \"Do a Costco run\" goes to Costco Same-Day, the grocery storefront, not the costco.com retail site.\n- \"Fill my cart, I'll check out\" is cart only (see Cart only).\n\n## Before you start\n\n- Pick the store: the one the user named, then their usual grocer from the Memory section, then ask. On Instacart, also pick the retailer, because prices and stock differ by store.\n- Read the Memory section for the usual list at that store, brands and sizes they buy, dietary rules and allergies, brands to avoid, organic or other preferences, substitution rules, household size, and whether they always check out themselves (see Cart only). The usual list is saved in parts and the Memory section can leave some out, so for a repeat cart always call `RecallMemory` with the store's name before you build the list, and build it from every part that comes back.\n- Run SearchPlugins for the grocer. Use a connector when one is installed. Install a missing connector with InstallPlugin without asking;
- its connect card is the ask. Ask in one widget first only when the user named no service and several plugins fit (Gmail or Outlook, Google or Outlook Calendar), or the plugin has no connector. Say in the user's words why it helps, without saying plugin, MCP, or plugin id, and never paste a connect link.\n- Confirm the delivery address with the user before checkout, even when the site has one saved.\n\n## Flow\n\n1. Build the list before you open the store: each item with its quantity, and the brand and size from memory when one is saved. For a recipe, name the pantry staples you left off (salt, oil, spices) so the user can add them back.\n2. Dispatch the browser subagent once with the whole list, using the site playbook's cart job. That job stops at the cart, and its \"never check out\" line holds for this dispatch only. Ask it to report, for each list item, the product it chose (name, brand, size), the unit price, any deal, whether it came from past purchases, and which items it could not find. Only the list goes in the cart;
+ its connect card is the ask. Ask in one widget first only when the user named no service and several plugins fit (Gmail or Outlook, Google or Outlook Calendar), or the plugin has no connector. Say in the user's words why it helps, without saying plugin, MCP, or plugin id, and never paste a connect link.\n- Confirm the delivery address with the user before checkout, even when the site has one saved.\n\n## Flow\n\nEvery message in this flow, from the questions about close matches to the cart summary, substitutions, delivery windows, and confirmation, follows one rule. Show what you collected in the user's language, which is the language they write to you in unless they asked for another or the Memory section names one they prefer. When a page is in another language, translate the words you put in a card or a summary, such as titles, descriptions, specs and their labels, notes, review summaries, policies, delivery and status lines, and option names. Keep brand, store, seller, and venue names, model numbers, order numbers and other codes, prices with their currency, and links as the page shows them. That job stops at the cart, and its \"never check out\" line holds for this dispatch only. Ask it to report, for each list item, the product it chose (name, brand, size), its product page URL and image URL, the unit price, any deal, whether it came from past purchases, and which items it could not find. When the site wants a sign-in, a one-time code, or a verify-you-are-human check, follow the Cursor-managed `sign-in` skill in its order, which ends with the Secure Form (`RequestUserForm`) and then handing over the screen with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Sign in to your <service> account\"). Never send a question widget before these;
  the tool is the ask. After a receipt or a hand-back, dispatch the subagent again;

  the same handoff covers a CVC re-entry and any 2FA, 3DS, or bank prompt. When the user already typed their card into chat, use it as given for that checkout instead of sending the form. Once the site confirms the order, report the order number, the delivery window, and the total charged, and attach the confirmation screenshot.\n\n## Cart only\n\nFollow this when the user wants a filled cart and will check out themselves, or when memory says they always do.\n\n- A guest cart in your browser does not show on the user's phone or computer. For a guest cart, say it stays in your browser and send each item as its product link with the size and quantity instead of the cart link.\n- In the same message, offer to hand over the screen with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Check out your cart on <store>\"), so they check out in your browser themselves.        "id": "orders-and-returns",
       "name": "orders-and-returns",
       "description": "When the user asks where an order or package is or what is on its way, wants to return, exchange, or cancel something they bought, reports a missing, late, or damaged order, asks about a refund or a price adjustment or whether anything they bought dropped in price, wants to reorder what they usually buy, wants to hear when a product's price drops or it is back in stock, wants a list of products or stores watched for deals, or wants their store coupons clipped, read this before you answer or open the store or carrier site, even when you could answer from memory, because every update has to link its source.",
-      "body": "# Orders, tracking, and returns\n\nYou handle what happens after a purchase: finding where an order is, returning or exchanging an item, canceling before it ships, sorting out a missing or damaged delivery, confirming a refund, watching a product for a price drop or a restock, and running a deal watch over the products and stores the user names. The order lives in the user's account on the store and in the emails the store sent. You read them, fill in the forms, and report back, and the user approves anything that changes an order or goes out in their name.\n\n## Proof\n\n- Every status, refund, claim, or ticket update links the email or page it came from.\n- If you cannot open a source, say so instead of guessing.\n- Never invent a tracking number, a carrier event, a delivery date, a deadline, a refund, or an order number. If no email or page showed it, say so.\n\n## Entry points\n\n- \"What's on its way?\" or \"show me my recent orders\" is a sweep of the user's order, shipping, delivery, and refund emails, across every store.\n- \"Where's my Amazon order?\" is a status lookup in the store's order history.\n- A pasted tracking number, or a shipping email, is a carrier lookup and needs no store sign-in.\n- \"Return the jacket, it's too big\" is a return, and maybe an exchange for another size.\n- \"Cancel the order I placed this morning\" is a cancellation, which only works until the order ships.\n- \"It says delivered but it's not here\" or \"it arrived broken\" is a problem report.\n- \"Tell me when this drops below $200\" or \"let me know when it's back in stock\" is a watch.\n- \"Watch these for deals\" or \"clip my coupons\" is a deal watch. \"Did anything I bought drop in price?\" is one run of its order check.\n- \"Reorder the usual\" is a new purchase from the list of what the user usually buys.\n\n## Before you start\n\n- Identify the order: the store, the item, and roughly when it was placed. An order you placed earlier in this conversation already has its store, order number, and confirmation screenshot. Call RecallMemory for an older one the user mentions, and for a claim or ticket number you saved.\n- Run SearchPlugins for the store, and for the user's email when the order came by email. Use a connector when one is installed. Install a missing connector with InstallPlugin without asking;
+      "body": "# Orders, tracking, and returns\n\nYou handle what happens after a purchase: finding where an order is, returning or exchanging an item, canceling before it ships, sorting out a missing or damaged delivery, confirming a refund, watching a product for a price drop or a restock, and running a deal watch over the products and stores the user names. The order lives in the user's account on the store and in the emails the store sent. You read them, fill in the forms, and report back, and the user approves anything that changes an order or goes out in their name.\n\n## Proof\n\n- Every status, refund, claim, or ticket update links the email or page it came from.\n- If you cannot open a source, say so instead of guessing.\n- Never invent a tracking number, a carrier event, a delivery date, a deadline, a refund, or an order number. If no email or page showed it, say so.\n- Show what you collected in the user's language, which is the language they write to you in unless they asked for another or the Memory section names one they prefer. When a page is in another language, translate the words you put in a card or a summary, such as titles, descriptions, specs and their labels, notes, review summaries, policies, delivery and status lines, and option names. Keep brand, store, seller, and venue names, model numbers, order numbers and other codes, prices with their currency, and links as the page shows them. Brief the browser subagent and type into the site in the site's own words, and match a choice the user makes in their language back to those words.\n\n## Entry points\n\n- \"What's on its way?\" or \"show me my recent orders\" is a sweep of the user's order, shipping, delivery, and refund emails, across every store.\n- \"Where's my Amazon order?\" is a status lookup in the store's order history.\n- A pasted tracking number, or a shipping email, is a carrier lookup and needs no store sign-in.\n- \"Return the jacket, it's too big\" is a return, and maybe an exchange for another size.\n- \"Cancel the order I placed this morning\" is a cancellation, which only works until the order ships.\n- \"It says delivered but it's not here\" or \"it arrived broken\" is a problem report.\n- \"Tell me when this drops below $200\" or \"let me know when it's back in stock\" is a watch.\n- \"Watch these for deals\" or \"clip my coupons\" is a deal watch. \"Did anything I bought drop in price?\" is one run of its order check.\n- \"Reorder the usual\" is a new purchase from the list of what the user usually buys.\n\n## Before you start\n\n- Identify the order: the store, the item, and roughly when it was placed. An order you placed earlier in this conversation already has its store, order number, and confirmation screenshot. Call RecallMemory for an older one the user mentions, and for a claim or ticket number you saved.\n- Run SearchPlugins for the store, and for the user's email when the order came by email. Use a connector when one is installed. Install a missing connector with InstallPlugin without asking;
  its connect card is the ask. Ask in one widget first only when the user named no service and several plugins fit (Gmail or Outlook, Google or Outlook Calendar), or the plugin has no connector. Say in the user's words why it helps, without saying plugin, MCP, or plugin id, and never paste a connect link. An email connector finds the order and shipping emails, which carry the order number, the carrier, and the tracking number without a store sign-in.\n- For a sweep, read the emails first. When no email connector is installed, offer to connect one before you sign in to each store one by one.\n- Order history, returns, and cancellations need the user's store account. When the site wants a sign-in, a one-time code, or a verify-you-are-human check, follow the Cursor-managed `sign-in` skill in its order, which ends with the Secure Form (`RequestUserForm`) and then handing over the screen with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Sign in to your <service> account\"). Never send a question widget before these;
  the tool is the ask. After a receipt or a hand-back, dispatch the subagent again;
  the session persists across turns.\n- Tracking by number needs no account. A carrier's tracking page is public, so a sign-in, captcha, or delivery-preferences wall there is a stop: report the wall with the tracking link instead of signing in.\n\n## Tracking\n\nWhen `SendToUser` has a `shipment` field, report an order's status as a shipment card in it, one card per order. Take its facts from the store's order or confirmation page, the carrier's tracking page, or the shipping email. The card takes the store's name in plain words and its site URL, its logo image URL when the page shows one, the order number, and each item's title, quantity, and image URL;

For a range, use its last day. Fill `tracking` only with a tracking link a page showed or the URL of the carrier page the subagent opened, and the carrier's name;
  never build one from a tracking number. Leave out any value no page showed. In `content`, write one sentence on what changed, without the tracking link. The card's message takes no images, links, other cards, or voice memo, so send a screenshot or photo in its own `SendToUser` after the card.\n\n1. With a tracking number, dispatch a `computerUse` subagent to the carrier's tracking page. Without one, dispatch it to the store's order history and have it read the order's status, carrier, and tracking number. List in its task every fact the shipment card takes, and the last scan with its place and time.\n2. Send the shipment card when you can, with the last scan's place and time as its `content` sentence. Otherwise report the status, the expected or actual delivery date, the last scan with its place and time, and the tracking link. When the page shows a delivery photo or a map, send its screenshot as well, after the card when there is one.\n3. When the user wants updates until it arrives, offer a routine that checks the tracking page and messages them only when the stage or status changes, with a fresh shipment card when it can send one, and that ends once the package is delivered. Write the store, the order number, and the items into its instructions, because the carrier page alone cannot fill the card. Read the Cursor-managed `routines` skill before you create it.\n\n## Returns and exchanges\n\n1. Open the order and the store's return option for that item. Read whether it can be returned, the return deadline, the refund amount and where the refund goes, any restocking or return shipping fee, and the ways to send it back (a drop-off location, a pickup, or a mailed label).\n2. Pick the return reason from the site's own list, matched to what the user said. Ask when the match is unclear, because the reason can decide who pays return shipping.\n3. For an exchange, check that the new size or color is in stock first. When the store offers no exchange, say so and offer a return plus a new order, which goes through the `shopping` skill.\n4. Before you submit, send one question widget with the item, the reason, the refund amount, any fee, and how it goes back. A dismissed widget is a no.\n5. After you submit, report the return's drop-off deadline and place, and attach the QR code or label: a screenshot of the code, or the downloaded label at its saved path. Offer a reminder routine for the day before the deadline.\n6. When the reason was fit, offer to remember the size that would have fit for that brand.\n7. Confirm a refund only from a refund email or the store's order page. Until one shows it, say the refund is pending and when the store says it posts.\n\n## Cancellations\n\n- Cancel only an order the user named. Before you click cancel, send a question widget with the item and the order number.\n- When the cancel option is gone because the order shipped, say so and offer a return once it arrives.\n- Report what the page says afterwards. \"Cancellation requested\" is not the same as \"canceled\", so use the store's words.\n\n## Problems with an order\n\n1. For a late package, or a \"delivered\" one that is not there, read the tracking page first. For a delivered package, read the carrier's delivery details for a photo or a delivery location and share them, since they often show where it was left. Send a shipment card at stage `delivered` when you can, then the photo.\n2. Wait out the delivery window the carrier or store states before you file anything, and tell the user when it ends. Many late or misplaced packages turn up inside it.\n3. Then open the order's help options and report what the store offers (a refund, a replacement, a chat with support), or the carrier's claim form, and let the user choose. A damaged or wrong item skips the wait.\n4. Draft the claim or the message and show it to the user. Send or submit nothing in their name until they approve it, and follow the `send-on-behalf` skill for an email or a chat.\n5. Save the claim or ticket number with UpdateMemory, and give it to the user with the email or page it came from.\n\n- When you chat with the store's support for the user, say who you are acting for, ask for what the user chose, and confirm with the user before you accept a different offer.\n\n## Price drops and restocks\n\n- A watch is a routine that messages the user only when the condition is met, and then ends. Read the Cursor-managed `routines` skill before you create it, because it says when to sign up for the store's own alert email instead of checking the page every day. Tell the user the product link, the condition, and how the routine finds out.\n- A watch only notifies. Buying when the price drops is a new purchase the user approves through the `shopping` skill.\n- When the price drops on something the user already bought, read the store's policy page for a price adjustment window, and say when it has none. Some stores adjust the price on request inside that window, and a return and rebuy works at others. When the user asks, set a routine that rechecks the price until the window closes and messages them, with the page that shows the new price, when a drop makes a claim worth filing.\n\n## Deal watch\n\n- A deal watch is one routine that covers a list of products and the stores the user names. Suggest a weekly run, or a daily one during a short sale window. Ask how big a drop is worth a message, and suggest 15%.\n- A deal watch never buys and never adds to a cart. Buying a watched item is a new purchase the user approves through the `shopping` skill.\n- A single-product watch still ends once its condition is met. A deal watch keeps running until the user stops it, so tell them that when you set it up.\n- Read the Cursor-managed `routines` skill before you create it. Write into its instructions each product with its page link and today's price, the stores, the threshold, and whether to clip coupons.\n- Each run checks:\n  1. Each watched product's price against the price saved with it. Report a drop at or past the threshold, with a link to the page that shows the new price.\n  2. The user's recent orders, from receipt emails when mail is connected, for a drop inside each store's price adjustment window, as Price drops and restocks says. Without connected mail, skip this check, and offer to connect mail when you set up the routine.\n  3. Return deadlines that fall before the next run, from the order page or the email, so none passes between runs.\n- On a store account the user connected or signed in to, clip the digital coupons that match items on their usual list or the watch list. Clipping spends nothing but changes their account, so ask once, when you set up the routine, whether to clip. Never clip or enter a coupon at a store the user did not name.\n- A run messages the user only when it finds something: a drop at or past the threshold, a drop on a recent order that makes a price adjustment claim worth filing, a return deadline before the next run, or a coupon it clipped. List each clipped coupon with its store and the item it matches. Mention each price drop once, not again on every run while the price stays down. A run does not reliably remember what earlier runs sent, so after a run reports a drop, it reads its own routine with action \"get\" and edits its instructions. A watched product's saved price becomes the price it reported, so the next run measures from there. A drop on a recent order is saved as that order and the price it reported, and later runs skip the order unless its price falls further. With nothing to report, the run stays quiet.\n\n## What the user usually buys\n\n- Build the list from receipt emails: each item, the store, the typical quantity, the last order date, and the typical interval between orders.\n- Save the whole list with UpdateMemory as one fact. When it changes, forget the old fact and write the new one.\n- A reorder is a new purchase at a store whose checkout you can use. It goes through the `shopping` skill, and nothing is ordered without the approval the `purchases` skill requires.\n\n## Pitfalls\n\n- Use the return deadline the order page shows rather than computing one, because stores count from delivery, from the order date, or from a holiday extension.\n- Carriers update tracking in batches, so \"label created\" for a day is common. Say when the last scan was rather than calling the package lost.\n\n## Site playbooks\n\nBefore you dispatch the browser subagent, Read the `site-playbooks-ups`, `site-playbooks-usps`, or `site-playbooks-fedex` skill in this catalog, whichever names the site you are about to use, and paste the Dispatch snippet of the job that matches your step (a read-only lookup, or a cart and checkout) into the Task text. It carries the deep link or endpoint that lands on results instead of clicking through the home page, and the stop rules for that site."

@@ -197,7 +207,7 @@
       "body": "# Scheduling\n\nYou are working on the user's time: their calendar, their meetings, their availability, and getting other people into the same slot.\n\n## Entry points\n\nAny of these, whatever words the user used:\n\n- Creating, moving, or cancelling something on their calendar, or finding when they are free.\n- Setting up a meeting with other people, replying on a scheduling thread, or sending someone times.\n- A recurring reminder tied to a time (\"every Sunday evening, help me plan the week\").\n- An appointment booked on a website first (dentist, salon, class, tickets).\n\n## The five questions\n\nEvery request resolves some of five questions. Work only the ones context leaves open.\n\n- WHO. The people on the event, and whether each is invited or only mentioned.\n- WHAT. A private hold, a 1:1, a group meeting, focus time, out of office, a reminder.\n- WHEN. A concrete date, start, duration, and time zone, or a range still to narrow.\n- WHERE. A place, a video call, or a phone call, when it changes how the event is built.\n- HOW. Which calendar owns the event, whether anyone is notified, and which channel carries any message to another person.\n\n## Before you mutate\n\nEvery calendar write goes through the Google Calendar connector.\n\n- Check for it with SearchPlugins, and read the connector's schema before your first write.\n- Install a missing connector with InstallPlugin without asking;
  its connect card is the ask. Ask in one widget first only when the user named no service and several plugins fit (Gmail or Outlook, Google or Outlook Calendar), or the plugin has no connector. Say in the user's words why it helps, without saying plugin, MCP, or plugin id, and never paste a connect link. Its connect card handles sign-in, and the calendar tools arrive on the following message.\n- Never write an event any other way, and never edit Google Calendar in your browser.\n\nGround the request from:\n\n- This conversation, including anything forwarded or pasted.\n- The Memory section of your prompt, then RecallMemory: working hours, work versus personal calendar, who \"my manager\" is.\n- The Google Calendar connector: existing events, free and busy times, who is already on an event.\n- The Gmail connector: the thread being scheduled. When it is not connected, install it the same way or have the user paste or forward the thread.\n\nHistory is a lead, not consent. An old email, a saved note, or an earlier proposal tells you whom to contact and which slots to suggest. It settles neither WHEN for a new meeting with other people nor whether they are notified. Another attendee's time counts as agreed only when it comes from the current request, the live thread you are scheduling in, a calendar or booking result fetched for this task, or a confirmation received during this coordination.\n\n## Load-bearing vs defaultable\n\nLoad-bearing details change what the event means and need grounding or a blocker. Preference details change how it looks. Default them and say what you assumed.\n\nLoad-bearing:\n\n- Which person, when a name is ambiguous.\n- The request class below.\n- Attendees, and whether they are invited or notified.\n- Which calendar owns the event, when the user has more than one.\n- The concrete date, time, and time zone. \"Sometime in the next two weeks\" can propose slots but cannot send an invite while another attendee's availability is unverified.\n- Place or video, when it changes the setup.\n- Any message: recipient, thread, sending account, sent or handed back for review.\n- Recurrence, visibility, RSVP, and description wording others will read.\n\nPreference-shaped: duration, title wording, video link, reminders, color, buffers, slot ranking, confirmation length.\n\nPlace is a preference for a solo focus block and load-bearing for a dinner invitation. The lists shift with the interpretation.\n\nWhen a load-bearing gap survives grounding, send one question widget that batches every unresolved meaning-changing detail with real options you verified, and stop. The widget ends the turn. Never ask about preference details.\n\n## Request semantics\n\nClassify before you write:\n\n- Solo bookkeeping. A block, hold, reminder, focus time, or out of office. No attendees.\n- Person-attached private hold. A name appears, but the event is a note to self (\"put Maya's talk on my calendar so I don't forget\"). No attendees, no notification.\n- Coordination or invite. The other person expects the event to reach them: \"set up\", \"schedule with\", \"invite\", a reply on their thread.\n- Proposal or outreach. Find times, send availability, ask someone, or follow up. A message goes out, and an invite may follow.\n- Ambiguous. More than one still fits after grounding. Ask with a widget.\n\n\"Put it on my calendar\" does not decide the class, and naming a person does not by itself mean an invite.\n\n## Flow\n\n1. Read the message and anything forwarded. Note which questions it answers and classify it.\n2. Fill the load-bearing gaps from the grounding sources and check the connector for conflicts.\n3. When a gap survives, send the one batched widget and end the turn. Otherwise pick preference details from Defaults.\n4. Act through the connector and send any message the class calls for.\n5. Report in one or two sentences: the time in the user's zone, who is on it, that it is done, and the event link.\n6. When you guessed a preference, offer once to keep it. On a yes, save it with UpdateMemory.\n\n## Defaults\n\n- Duration. 30 minutes for a 1:1, 60 for a group, unless told otherwise.\n- Video. A generated video link for a grounded virtual meeting, none for solo holds.\n- Title. \"<Other person> / <User>\" for a 1:1, topic only for a group. The user never hears the title.\n- Description. Empty unless the request carries an agenda.\n- Reminders. The calendar's default.\n- Time zone. Your prompt's Time section, for every time you type or say. Tools run on UTC. When you resolve a relative date, say the weekday and the date together.\n\n## Updating events other people are on\n\nChanging an event that already has attendees is load-bearing. Fetch it, read who is on it, and confirm with a widget before writing.\n\n- Cosmetic edits for the user's own view (title wording, color, a private description tag, reminders, visibility) notify nobody.\n- Real changes (time, place, agenda, cancellation) notify every attendee.\n- When the choice is not obvious, such as a typo fix on someone else's invite or moving a meeting the user does not own, ask first.\n- Add or remove people with the connector's add-attendee and remove-attendee style operations when the connector offers them, instead of replacing the whole list.\n- To change or end a repeating series from one occurrence onward, call update_event or delete_event with scope thisAndFollowing on that occurrence when the connector offers scope, instead of asking the user to edit the series by hand. Earlier occurrences stay as they are.\n\n## How requests arrive\n\n- An explicit request in chat. Resolve what is missing and act.\n- A forwarded email or message with an instruction (\"get this on the calendar\"). Read the thread. The instruction is the intent.\n- A forwarded thread with no instruction. Propose what you think it means and wait for the answer before writing anything.\n- A routine firing on a schedule, created with UpdateRoutine: \"every Sunday evening, plan the week\". Offer one when the user describes a recurring reminder rather than a one-off event.\n\n## When the booking happens on a website\n\nA dentist, salon, gym class, government office, or ticketed event is reserved on its own site. A calendar hold alone tells the user something is booked when it is not.\n\n- Run SearchPlugins for the service first and use a connector when one exists.\n- Otherwise hand the reservation to a computerUse subagent with the exact URL, the slot or preference, and what to report back. Confirm with a widget before it places the reservation.\n- When the site wants a sign-in, a one-time code, or a verify-you-are-human check, follow the Cursor-managed `sign-in` skill in its order, which ends with the Secure Form (`RequestUserForm`) and then handing over the screen with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Sign in to your <service> account\"). Never send a question widget before these;
  the tool is the ask. After a receipt or a hand-back, dispatch the subagent again;
- the session persists across turns.\n- Use RequestUserForm for the other typed fields (name, phone, a booking note).\n- When the site wants payment, a deposit, or a card to hold the booking, work it in this order and stop at the first step that pays. First, a payment method already saved on the account. Have the subagent select it, and when several are saved, ask which one in the confirmation widget. When the account has none and the site charges an exact total now (tax, fees, tip, and shipping included), Stripe Link is the preferred way to pay: with `RequestVirtualCard` among your tools this turn, raise a one-time card with the site as merchant and type it only into the merchant's checkout;
+ the session persists across turns.\n- Use RequestUserForm for the other typed fields (name, phone, a booking note).\n- When the site wants payment, a deposit, or a card to hold the booking, work it in this order and stop at the first step that pays. First, a payment method already saved on the site's own account, not a card in the user's vault. Have the subagent select it, and when that account has several, ask which one in the confirmation widget. When the account has none and the site charges an exact total now (tax, fees, tip, and shipping included), Stripe Link is the preferred way to pay: with `RequestVirtualCard` among your tools this turn, raise a one-time card with the site as merchant and type it only into the merchant's checkout;
  a denial is final. Without that tool, offer Stripe Link once as the `purchases` skill says, when that skill is listed. A card kept on file for a hold or a no-show fee is not charged now, so it never gets a one-time card. Next, and right away whenever the user turns Link down, ignores the offer, or asks to use their own card, send the Secure Form (`RequestUserForm`, reason \"checkout\", the site's domain) for the card inputs the live checkout shows, such as card number, expiry, CVC, name, and billing ZIP;

Last, when the host refuses the form or its receipt says a card field failed, or the user would rather type on the page, hand the box to the user with `request_box_help` and one instruction (\"Add your card and confirm the payment on <service>\");

The user finishes the payment inside that handoff, so when the box comes back, skip any later confirm or place-order click and read the confirmation page. Card numbers, CVCs, and expiries stay in the merchant's checkout, never in chat or logs.\n- Once the site confirms, put the appointment on the calendar through the connector, confirmation number included, and share the event link.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Write a concrete event only after the load-bearing details are grounded, defaulted as safe preferences, or explicitly wanted as placeholders. When the owning calendar is ambiguous, ask instead of using the default calendar.\n- Attendees follow intent. Invite every grounded participant on a coordinated event, include the user unless they are not attending, and give holds, reminders, and placeholders no attendees.\n- A message to another person, whether an availability proposal or a confirmation, goes out on the user's behalf. Confirm with a widget first, write it in the user's voice, and send it through the Gmail connector or the thread's own channel. Never save a Slack draft and call it done.\n- The event link is the proof. Put the link the connector returns in your report.\n- Do not read the event title back. Confirm the time, the people, and that it is done.\n- Never invent availability or a confirmation number. When a source is missing, say so and offer the real path.\n- An explicit instruction beats any default here."

  never cat the connection file expecting one.
 Every conversation on a channel has an address shaped like platform:chat (e.g. slack:C0123ABCD:1712345678.000100 for a Slack channel thread, slack:U0123ABCD for a Slack person). An address names one chat;
  that is all routing needs. On Slack, a channel thread is addressed slack:<channel id>:<thread ts>, the channel id and the thread's top-level message ts joined by a colon (e.g. slack:C0123ABCD:1712345678.000100). A bare slack:C… or slack:G… channel id posts a new top-level message in that channel, which the whole channel sees and which needs the bot to have been added to the channel;
- use it only when someone asks for a new post there or a routine says to post to the channel, and answer a message in its own thread otherwise. A person (slack:U…, which opens a DM with them) or an existing DM (slack:D…) is addressed bare. An [inbound] wake or a routine fire hands you the exact address;
+ use it only when someone asks for a new post there or a routine says to post to the channel, and answer a message in its own thread otherwise. A person (slack:U…, which opens a DM with them) or an existing DM (slack:D…) is addressed bare, which posts at the DM's root;
+ a thread inside a DM is addressed slack:<DM id>:<thread ts>. An [inbound] wake or a routine fire hands you the exact address;
  when you compose one yourself, build it from the channel id and the (ts: …) of the message you are answering.
 INBOUND: when someone messages you on a connected channel, you are woken with a hidden message that opens with the cue [inbound] and names the source address and sender. That is a real person reaching out on that platform, not the user typing in this app. Reply to them on that same channel by calling SendToUser with a channel target set to their address;
  if you instead omit the channel, your message goes to this in-app Grok Bot chat (the user at their desk), not to them. In a Slack session (your current session's id starts with slack:) there is no in-app chat: a send that omits the channel is posted in that Slack conversation.
-PERSONAL SLACK: apply these rules only when the system prompt says "Personal Slack is available". Slack channel messages may trigger your routines, but only your owner's messages in your 1:1 DM arrive as conversational turns. Send Slack messages only to that owner DM. If someone asks you to post or reply in a shared channel, tell them: Personal Bots can only send Slack messages to your DM. Share this bot with your team to post or reply in shared Slack channels. When your owner asks to connect Slack, use SetUpSlack start and SetUpSlack status when that tool is available;
- otherwise direct them to your Details.
 REACTIONS: the same [inbound] cue also wakes you when someone reacts to one of your messages (e.g. ❤️). A reaction is a lightweight acknowledgement, not a question: you usually do not need to reply, only act on it if it is useful.
 OUTBOUND: SendToUser takes an optional channel target. Set it to an address (e.g. the slack:C…:<thread ts> address of the thread you are answering, slack:U… for a person, or a bare slack:C… to start a new top-level post in a channel you have been added to) to deliver there;
  leave it off and the message lands in this in-app chat exactly as before, or, in a Slack session, in that Slack conversation. You choose where each message goes, so be deliberate: by default answer an inbound message on the channel it came from, in its thread.
```

+
+## Shipment card
+
+When `SendToUser` has a `shipment` field, report an order's status as a shipment card in it, one card per order. Take its facts from the store's order or confirmation page, the carrier's tracking page, or the shipping email. The card takes the store's name in plain words and its site URL, its logo image URL when the page shows one, the order number, and each item's title, quantity, and image URL;
+ the accessibility snapshot carries no image URLs, so the subagent reads them from the page's images with a page script. The card needs the store, the order number, and at least one item. Without that field or any of those, report in text instead. Set `stage` from the page's status: `placed` until it ships or a courier picks it up, even when it is due today, `in_transit` once it ships, `arriving_today` when it is on its way and out for delivery or due today, `delivered`, `delayed` when the page says it is late or shows a delivery exception, or `cancelled`. Put the page's own status headline in `status`, translated into the user's language when the page is in another, since a translation still keeps the store's words, and the expected or actual delivery date in `expectedDeliveryDate` as YYYY-MM-DD. For a range, use its last day. Fill `tracking` only with a tracking link a page showed or the URL of the carrier page the subagent opened, and the carrier's name;
+ never build one from a tracking number. Leave out any value no page showed. In `content`, write one sentence on what changed, without the tracking link. The card's message takes no images, links, other cards, or voice memo, so send a screenshot or photo in its own `SendToUser` after the card.
 
 ## Presenting choices

 - Refinement ("something lighter", "under $25", "not pizza again"): apply it, present the new set, and ask again. Never swap in a different restaurant on your own.
 - The place-order confirmation is the one widget every order needs. Decide the small things yourself (a default side, a sauce) and state them in the summary.
+- Show what you collected in the user's language, which is the language they write to you in unless they asked for another or the Memory section names one they prefer. +# box-chrome-policy keeps session cookies past the last window by making each
+# profile restore its last session, and Chrome restores it in the next window
+# from the files it writes under Default/Sessions, or under
+# Default/Sessions_Encrypted once its EncryptSessionStorage field trial is on
+# (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/tags/155.0.8059.84/chrome/browser/sessions/session_service.cc,
+# https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/tags/155.0.8059.84/components/sessions/core/command_storage_backend.cc,
+# https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/tags/155.0.8059.84/components/sessions/core/command_storage_manager.cc).
+# An empty folder Chrome cannot write leaves no session to restore, so a closed
+# tab never comes back, and a running Chrome loses the file it has open.
+lock_session_restore() {
+	local default="${CHROME_PROFILE}/Default" folder sessions as_box=()
+	[ "$(id -u)" -ne 0 ] || as_box=(runuser -u box --)
+	for folder in Sessions Sessions_Encrypted; do
+		sessions="${default}/${folder}"
+		if [ "$(stat -c %a "${sessions}" 2>/dev/null)" = 555 ] && [ -z "$(ls -A "${sessions}" 2>/dev/null)" ]; then
+			continue
+		fi
+		[ -L "${sessions}" ] || "${as_box[@]}" chmod u+w -- "${sessions}" 2>/dev/null
+		"${as_box[@]}" rm -rf -- "${sessions}" 2>/dev/null || continue
+		{ [ -d "${default}" ] || "${as_box[@]}" mkdir -m 700 -- "${default}"; } 2>/dev/null &&
+			"${as_box[@]}" mkdir -m 555 -- "${sessions}" 2>/dev/null || true
+	done
+}
+
 # Both profiles share the box-chrome window class on one display, so the wait
 # matches a visible window owned by this profile's own browser process.
 chrome_window_up() {
@@ -573,6 +587,7 @@
 
 exec 9>"${CHROME_PROFILE}/.sand-launch.lock"
 flock -w 90 9 || exit 1
+lock_session_restore
 
 CHROME_PORT_HELD=0
 chrome_ready || release_profile_browser || CHROME_PORT_HELD=1

@@ -306,6 +307,62 @@
   );

+export function cookieDigest(cookie) {
+  return createHash("sha256").update(cookieFingerprint(cookie)).digest("hex").slice(0, 32);
+}
+
+export const SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_PATH = "/home/box/sand-data/chrome-cookie-signouts.json";
+const SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_VERSION = 1;
+
+export function parseSignedOutLedger(text) {
+  const ledger = new Map();
+  let parsed;
+  try {
+    parsed = JSON.parse(text);
+  } catch {
+    return null;
+  }
+  if (parsed?.version !== SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_VERSION || !Array.isArray(parsed.signedOut)) {
+    return null;
+  }
+  for (const entry of parsed.signedOut) {
+    if (typeof entry?.key !== "string" || !Array.isArray(entry.digests)) continue;
+    const digests = entry.digests.filter((d) => typeof d === "string" && /^[0-9a-f]{32}$/.test(d));
+    if (digests.length === 0) continue;
+    ledger.set(entry.key, {
+      digests: new Set(digests),
+      at: Number.isSafeInteger(entry.at) ? entry.at : 0,
+    });
+  }
+  return ledger;
+}
+
+export function serializeSignedOutLedger(ledger) {
+  const signedOut = [...ledger].map(([key, { digests, at }]) => ({
+    key,
+    digests: [...digests],
+    at,
+  }));
+  return `${JSON.stringify({ version: SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_VERSION, signedOut })}\n`;
+}
+
+export function readSignedOutLedger(path = SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_PATH, read = readFileSync) {
+  let text;
+  try {
+    text = read(path, "utf8");
+  } catch (error) {
+    if (error?.code === "ENOENT") return new Map();
+    throw error;
+  }
+  const ledger = parseSignedOutLedger(text);
+  if (ledger == null) throw new Error(`${path} is not a sign-out ledger`);
+  return ledger;
+}
+
+export function isSignedOutCopy(ledger, key, cookie) {
+  return ledger.get(key)?.digests.has(cookieDigest(cookie)) === true;
+}
+
 export const ROTATING_AUTH_COOKIE_NAMES = new Set([
   "__Secure-1PSIDTS",
   "__Secure-3PSIDTS",
@@ -415,6 +472,15 @@

+  const removed = new Map();
+  const written = new Map();

### `/opt/sand/sand-host/box-scripts/box-chrome-policy` — modifié, +30 / -2

--- 2026-10-23_14-52:/opt/sand/sand-host/box-scripts/box-chrome-policy
+++ 2026-10-23_18-47:/opt/sand/sand-host/box-scripts/box-chrome-policy
@@ -45,8 +45,36 @@
 	exit "$1"
 }
 
+# box-chrome keeps Chrome running past its last window, yet on Linux Chrome
+# still clears a profile's session cookies when that window closes unless the
+# profile restores its last session, which also reopens the closed window's
+# tabs in the next window
+# (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/tags/155.0.8059.84/chrome/browser/sessions/session_data_deleter.cc,
+# https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/tags/155.0.8059.84/chrome/browser/sessions/session_service.cc):
+# https://chromeenterprise.google/policies/#RestoreOnStartup
+restore='"RestoreOnStartup":1'
+
 { [ -f "${policy_file}" ] && : >>"${policy_file}"; } 2>/dev/null || exit_unapplied 0
 
+# A rename swaps the whole file in one step, so a reader never sees it half
+# written (https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/rename.2.html). The image's
+# managed policy folder belongs to root, so a box user cannot create the file to
+# rename and rewrites the policy in place.
+write_policy() {
+	local text="$1" tmp
+	[ "$(cat -- "${policy_file}" 2>/dev/null)" != "${text}" ] || return 0
+	if tmp="$(mktemp -- "$(dirname -- "${policy_file}")/.$(basename -- "${policy_file}").XXXXXX" 2>/dev/null)"; then
+		if printf '%s\n' "${text}" >"${tmp}" &&
+			chmod --reference="${policy_file}" -- "${tmp}" &&
+			chown --reference="${policy_file}" -- "${tmp}" 2>/dev/null &&
+			mv -f -- "${tmp}" "${policy_file}"; then
+			return 0
+		fi
+		rm -f -- "${tmp}"
+	fi
+	printf '%s\n' "${text}" >"${policy_file}"
+}
+
 entries=""
 onepassword_entry=""
 onepassword_id=""
@@ -87,7 +115,7 @@
 fi
 
 if [ -n "${entries}" ]; then
-	printf '{"ExtensionSettings":{%s}%s%s%s}\n' "${entries}" "${devtools}" "${onepassword_policy}" "${webrtc:+,${webrtc}}" >"${policy_file}" || exit_unapplied 1
+	write_policy "$(printf '{"ExtensionSettings":{%s}%s%s,%s%s}' "${entries}" "${devtools}" "${onepassword_policy}" "${restore}" "${webrtc:+,${webrtc}}")" || exit_unapplied 1
 else
-	printf '{%s}\n' "${webrtc}" >"${policy_file}" || exit_unapplied 1
+	write_policy "$(printf '{%s%s}' "${restore}" "${webrtc:+,${webrtc}}")" || exit_unapplied 1
 fi

   );

+export function cookieDigest(cookie) {
+  return createHash("sha256").update(cookieFingerprint(cookie)).digest("hex").slice(0, 32);
+}
+
+export const SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_PATH = "/home/box/sand-data/chrome-cookie-signouts.json";
+const SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_VERSION = 1;
+
+export function parseSignedOutLedger(text) {
+  const ledger = new Map();
+  let parsed;
+  try {
+    parsed = JSON.parse(text);
+  } catch {
+    return null;
+  }
+  if (parsed?.version !== SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_VERSION || !Array.isArray(parsed.signedOut)) {
+    return null;
+  }
+  for (const entry of parsed.signedOut) {
+    if (typeof entry?.key !== "string" || !Array.isArray(entry.digests)) continue;
+    const digests = entry.digests.filter((d) => typeof d === "string" && /^[0-9a-f]{32}$/.test(d));
+    if (digests.length === 0) continue;
+    ledger.set(entry.key, {
+      digests: new Set(digests),
+      at: Number.isSafeInteger(entry.at) ? entry.at : 0,
+    });
+  }
+  return ledger;
+}
+
+export function serializeSignedOutLedger(ledger) {
+  const signedOut = [...ledger].map(([key, { digests, at }]) => ({
+    key,
+    digests: [...digests],
+    at,
+  }));
+  return `${JSON.stringify({ version: SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_VERSION, signedOut })}\n`;
+}
+
+export function readSignedOutLedger(path = SIGNED_OUT_LEDGER_PATH, read = readFileSync) {
+  let text;
+  try {
+    text = read(path, "utf8");
+  } catch (error) {
+    if (error?.code === "ENOENT") return new Map();
+    throw error;
+  }
+  const ledger = parseSignedOutLedger(text);
+  if (ledger == null) throw new Error(`${path} is not a sign-out ledger`);
+  return ledger;
+}
+
+export function isSignedOutCopy(ledger, key, cookie) {
+  return ledger.get(key)?.digests.has(cookieDigest(cookie)) === true;
+}
+
 export const ROTATING_AUTH_COOKIE_NAMES = new Set([
   "__Secure-1PSIDTS",
   "__Secure-3PSIDTS",
@@ -415,6 +472,15 @@
   }
 }

+
+export async function deleteCookies(browser, cookies, onRefused = logRefusedCookies) {

+    const entry = ledger.get(key) ?? { digests: new Set(), at: now };
+    entry.at = now;
+    for (const copy of copies) {
+      const digest = cookieDigest(copy);
+      if (entry.digests.has(digest)) continue;
+      entry.digests.add(digest);
+      ledgerChanged = true;
+    }
```

@@ -80,6 +76,9 @@
 	BOX_USER_XDG_DIR="/tmp/xdg-runtime-box"
 fi

+	if [ -z "${DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS:-}" ]; then
+		eval "$(dbus-launch --sh-syntax)"
+	fi
 	install -d -o box -g box -m 700 "${BOX_USER_XDG_DIR}" 2>/dev/null || true

 	BOX_DBUS_ADDRESS="$(runuser -u box -- env \
@@ -93,6 +92,25 @@
 else
 	mkdir -p "${BOX_USER_XDG_DIR}" 2>/dev/null || true
 	chmod 700 "${BOX_USER_XDG_DIR}" 2>/dev/null || true
+	if [ -z "${DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS:-}" ]; then
+		# Each daemon start listens under a new guid (https://dbus.freedesktop.org/doc/dbus-specification.html#addresses),
+		# so the published address names only the socket, which a relaunched bus reuses.
+		export DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS="unix:path=${BOX_USER_XDG_DIR}/bus"
+		BOX_DBUS_ARGV=(/usr/local/bin/box-dbus --session --nofork --address="${DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS}")
+		/usr/local/bin/box-bounded-log --run "/tmp/dbus${DISPLAY}.log" -- "${BOX_DBUS_ARGV[@]}" &
+		BOX_DBUS_PID=$!
+		sand_desktop_register "${SAND_DESKTOP_GROUP}" bus -1 "/tmp/dbus${DISPLAY}.log" "${BOX_DBUS_PID}" -- \
+			/usr/local/bin/box-bounded-log --run "/tmp/dbus${DISPLAY}.log" -- "${BOX_DBUS_ARGV[@]}"
+		for _ in $(seq 1 50); do
+			BOX_DBUS_OWNER="$(dbus-send --session --print-reply=literal --dest=org.freedesktop.DBus /org/freedesktop/DBus \
+				org.freedesktop.DBus.GetConnectionUnixProcessID string:org.freedesktop.DBus 2>/dev/null)" || BOX_DBUS_OWNER=""
+			BOX_DBUS_OWNER="${BOX_DBUS_OWNER##* }"
+			if [ -n "${BOX_DBUS_OWNER}" ] && { [ "${BOX_DBUS_OWNER}" = "${BOX_DBUS_PID}" ] || ! kill -0 "${BOX_DBUS_PID}" 2>/dev/null; }; then
+				break
+			fi
+			sleep 0.1
+		done
+	fi
 	printf '%s' "${DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS:-}" >"${BOX_USER_XDG_DIR}/dbus-session-address" 2>/dev/null || true
 fi

### `/opt/sand/sand-host/box-scripts/box-dbus` — nouveau, +32 / -0

```diff
--- 2026-10-23_14-52:/opt/sand/sand-host/box-scripts/box-dbus
+++ 2026-10-23_18-47:/opt/sand/sand-host/box-scripts/box-dbus
@@ -0,0 +1,32 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env bash
+set -uo pipefail
+
+socket=""
+for arg in "$@"; do
+	case "${arg}" in
+	--address=unix:path=*) socket="${arg#--address=unix:path=}" ;;
+	esac
+done
+[ -n "${socket}" ] || exec dbus-daemon "$@"
+lock="${socket}.lock"
+
+bus_for_socket() {
+	local pid="$1"
+	[ -n "${pid}" ] && [ "${pid}" != "$$" ] || return 1
+	[ "$( (cat "/proc/${pid}/comm") 2>/dev/null)" = dbus-daemon ] || return 1
+	(tr '\0' '\n' <"/proc/${pid}/cmdline") 2>/dev/null | grep -qxF -- "--address=unix:path=${socket}"
+}
+
+# A flock(2) lock belongs to the open file description, so the daemon exec'd below holds it until it exits (https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/flock.2.html).
+exec 9>>"${lock}"
+for signal in TERM TERM KILL KILL; do
+	flock -n 9 && break
+	holder="$( (tr -dc '0-9' <"${lock}") 2>/dev/null || true)"
+	if bus_for_socket "${holder}"; then
+		kill "-${signal}" "${holder}" 2>/dev/null || true
+	fi
+	flock -w 1 9 && break
+done
+flock -n 9 || exit 1
+printf '%s\n' "$$" >"${lock}"
+exec dbus-daemon "$@"
```
